# ADR-0315: ERP Coverage Doctrine - SAP-Parity Goal

## Status

Proposed - 2026-05-20. This ADR is doctrine for ERP parity and authorizes the nine Wave-3-G microservice scaffolds created with this brief.

2026-06-25 governance evidence for the ERP hyperscaler parity benchmark-surface gate is recorded at `evidence/multispectrum/waveA-erp-hyperscaler-benchmark-gate-20260625-1782430048.json`.

2026-06-25 corrective governance evidence for PR #860 blocker issues #865 and #866 is recorded at `evidence/multispectrum/waveA-erp-parity-blockers-20260625-1782433302.json`.

## §A Context

Enterprise resource planning remains one of the most valuable software categories in the world. SAP, Oracle, Microsoft, Workday, NetSuite, Sage Intacct, Infor, and IFS all monetize tenant lock-in around finance, procurement, supply chain, HR, operations, and reporting. SAP alone operates a $60B-plus business across ERP, procurement network, HCM, analytics, and platform extensions.
The legacy lock-in pattern is per-tenant suite gravity: financials, materials, warehouse, CRM, workforce, and analytics become one migration cliff. The oyatie advantage is the inverse: composable ERP through tenant-scoped Cedar authorization, workflow-engine orchestration, ontology object types, marketplace DealSet settlement, and flat microservices.
This doctrine targets SAP S/4HANA module parity without creating a monolithic ERP microservice. SAP S/4HANA, Oracle Fusion ERP Cloud, Microsoft Dynamics 365, Workday Financials, and NetSuite are benchmarks. The architecture remains ADR-0131 flat layout, ADR-0132 no-grouping, ADR-0244 tenant scoping, ADR-0245 substrate/product layering, ADR-0313 conglomerate tenant hierarchy, and ADR-0314 marketplace settlement.

### §A.1 ERP market shape
SAP S/4HANA bundles FI, CO, MM, SD, PP, QM, PM, HCM, PS, PLM, EHS, SRM, CRM, SCM/APO, GTS, TM, EWM, TRM, RE-FX, IS-* packs, network products, platform extensibility, and data analytics. Competitors distribute similar capabilities across cloud suites and acquisitions. oyatie expresses the same surface as a capability map across independently owned microservices.

### §A.2 Lock-in pattern
Incumbent ERP lock-in comes from shared identity, proprietary object models, proprietary extension languages, cross-module coupling, opaque workflow engines, and high-risk migrations. oyatie counters with tenant portability, ontology object mappings, connector-based import, workflow definitions in portable contracts, Cedar fragments, and DealSet settlement semantics.

## §B Decision

oyatie aims for full SAP S/4HANA module parity through composition. There will be no `microservices/erp/`, no ERP platform folder, and no single ownership boundary for all ERP surfaces. Each SAP module maps to a capability tier across existing and new flat microservices. Gaps are closed by adding single-concern microservices only where an SAP module introduces a distinct operational concern that cannot be cleanly owned by an existing service.

The first required new microservices are `production-planning`, `quality-management`, `plant-maintenance`, `warehouse`, `real-estate`, `crm`, `treasury`, `supply-chain-planning`, and `global-trade`. These nine raise the live filesystem count by nine and are reserved as Wave-3-G anchor stubs until full PR-143 artifact sets land.

## §C Consequences

| Engineering-rigor dimension | Doctrine consequence | Acceptance signal |
|---|---|---|
| Maintainability | ERP ownership remains per microservice, not suite-owned. | Every SAP module row has destination owners and no omnibus `erp` service. |
| Observability | ERP documents emit consistent audit, metric, trace, and log events. | Per-document audit-chain, object lineage, and SLO dashboards exist. |
| Scalability | Each module scales along its own bottleneck. | Warehouse scales by task volume; treasury by cash-position snapshots; CRM by account activity. |
| Performance | Module latency budgets are explicit and module-specific. | Query, mutation, and workflow p95/p99 targets are listed in PRD and service docs. |
| Optimization | Cost model is per capability and per tenant. | Finops receives cost center and DealSet/ERP object dimensions. |
| Code quality | Contracts use OpenAPI 3.2.0, AsyncAPI 3.1.0, proto3, BNF v4.1, and ADR-0105 layers. | Contract lint, property tests, replay tests, and migration fixtures pass. |

## §D Detailed Mechanics

### §D-1 Module-by-module mapping table

| SAP code | SAP module | SAP surfaces | oyatie destination | Status | Gap statement |

| FI | Financial Accounting | General Ledger; Accounts Receivable; Accounts Payable; Fixed Assets; Bank Accounting; Cash Management | specs/microservices/accounting.json, microservices/payments, microservices/finops-portal, microservices/treasury | partial-existing-plus-new-treasury | Accounting is spec-present but not a top-level scaffold in the live microservice tree; treasury fills advanced liquidity, debt, and FX gaps. |
| CO | Controlling | Cost Centers; Internal Orders; Profit Centers; CO-PA; Product Costing | microservices/finops-portal, microservices/ontology, microservices/workflow-engine, microservices/supply-chain-planning | covered-by-composition | Product costing becomes stronger after production-planning and supply-chain-planning publish cost object events. |

| SD | Sales and Distribution | Sales Orders; Pricing; Deliveries; Billing; Credit Management; Customer Master Data | microservices/marketplace, microservices/payments, microservices/crm, microservices/warehouse | partial-existing-plus-new-crm-and-warehouse | Customer master, quote-to-cash, credit, and service journeys require crm plus warehouse fulfillment events. |
| PP | Production Planning | BOM; MRP; Capacity Planning; Shop Floor; Routing | microservices/production-planning | new-required | No existing dedicated production planning microservice. |

### §D-1.A Detailed module notes

#### FI - Financial Accounting
- SAP surfaces covered: General Ledger, Accounts Receivable, Accounts Payable, Fixed Assets, Bank Accounting, Cash Management.
- oyatie destination: specs/microservices/accounting.json, microservices/payments, microservices/finops-portal, microservices/treasury.
- Current status: partial-existing-plus-new-treasury.
- Coverage rule: Financial Accounting must publish ontology object types, Cedar action namespaces, workflow templates, OpenAPI 3.2.0 commands, AsyncAPI 3.1.0 events, and proto3 internal calls where applicable.
- Audit rule: every FI business document emits create, amend, approve, post, reverse, archive, and export events when those states apply.
- Migration rule: SAP table extracts, Oracle exports, Workday reports, NetSuite CSV/API exports, and custom tenant imports map to the destination services through connect adapters.
- Parity gap: Accounting is spec-present but not a top-level scaffold in the live microservice tree; treasury fills advanced liquidity, debt, and FX gaps.

- Parity gap: No proprietary ABAP clone; extensibility uses SDK, Workflow Engine, Cedar fragments, and typed ontology actions.

#### DATA - Data and Analytics
- SAP surfaces covered: SAP Analytics Cloud, SAP Datasphere, SAP HANA.
- oyatie destination: microservices/analytics, microservices/ontology, microservices/intelligence, microservices/observability.
- Current status: covered-by-composition.
- Coverage rule: Data and Analytics must publish ontology object types, Cedar action namespaces, workflow templates, OpenAPI 3.2.0 commands, AsyncAPI 3.1.0 events, and proto3 internal calls where applicable.
- Audit rule: every DATA business document emits create, amend, approve, post, reverse, archive, and export events when those states apply.
- Migration rule: SAP table extracts, Oracle exports, Workday reports, NetSuite CSV/API exports, and custom tenant imports map to the destination services through connect adapters.
- Parity gap: Transactional ERP storage remains per-service; analytics uses projections rather than one HANA-style monolith.

#### microservices/production-planning/ naming justification
- Name: production-planning.
- SAP parity driver: PP.
- Single-concern boundary: plan and schedule manufacturing work from BOM through MRP, routing, capacity calendars, and shop-floor release.
- ADR-0132 no-grouping rationale: the service owns one ERP concern and composes adjacent concerns through workflow-engine, ontology, and events; it is not an ERP bundle.
- BNF v4.1 action namespace: production.planning.*
- ADR-0105 layer roster: api, rest, application, usecase, domain, kernel, adapter, worker, cli, test, contract, app, governance as needed.
- Hyperscaler comparators: SAP S/4HANA PP; Oracle Supply Chain Planning; Microsoft Dynamics 365 Supply Chain Management.
- Wave-3-G state: anchor scaffold now; full PR-143 artifact set follows after doctrine acceptance.

### §D-3 Per-module pack-overlay shape
A pack overlay activates Cedar fragments, ontology subclasses, workflow templates, validation rules, reporting views, cell-tier requirements, and jurisdictional evidence for one ERP module or industry variant. Example: `pack.is-banking` activates FI, CO, TRM, GTS, CRM, compliance, treasury, and audit-chain fragments while preserving sovereign child tenant scoping.
Pack overlays are not suite folders. They are deployable policy/configuration overlays that bind flat microservices together for a tenant and cell tier.

- Pack overlay for FI: activates specs/microservices/accounting.json, microservices/payments, microservices/finops-portal, microservices/treasury with module tag `FI` and keeps data ownership inside the destination services.
- Pack overlay for CO: activates microservices/finops-portal, microservices/ontology, microservices/workflow-engine, microservices/supply-chain-planning with module tag `CO` and keeps data ownership inside the destination services.
- Pack overlay for MM: activates microservices/marketplace, microservices/workflow-engine, microservices/connector, microservices/warehouse with module tag `MM` and keeps data ownership inside the destination services.
- Pack overlay for SD: activates microservices/marketplace, microservices/payments, microservices/crm, microservices/warehouse with module tag `SD` and keeps data ownership inside the destination services.
- Pack overlay for PP: activates microservices/production-planning with module tag `PP` and keeps data ownership inside the destination services.
- Pack overlay for QM: activates microservices/quality-management with module tag `QM` and keeps data ownership inside the destination services.
- Pack overlay for PM: activates microservices/plant-maintenance with module tag `PM` and keeps data ownership inside the destination services.
- Pack overlay for HCM: activates specs/microservices/hr.json, specs/microservices/payroll.json, docs/products/workplace-integration/PRD.md, microservices/workflow-engine with module tag `HCM` and keeps data ownership inside the destination services.
- Pack overlay for PS: activates microservices/workflow-engine, microservices/ontology, microservices/finops-portal, microservices/payments with module tag `PS` and keeps data ownership inside the destination services.
- Pack overlay for PLM: activates microservices/ontology, microservices/workflow-engine, microservices/connector, microservices/production-planning with module tag `PLM` and keeps data ownership inside the destination services.
- Pack overlay for EHS: activates microservices/compliance, microservices/workflow-engine, microservices/ontology, microservices/quality-management with module tag `EHS` and keeps data ownership inside the destination services.
- Pack overlay for SRM: activates microservices/marketplace, microservices/workflow-engine, microservices/ontology, microservices/payments with module tag `SRM` and keeps data ownership inside the destination services.
- Pack overlay for CRM: activates microservices/crm, microservices/community, microservices/marketplace, microservices/intelligence with module tag `CRM` and keeps data ownership inside the destination services.
- Pack overlay for SCM/APO: activates microservices/supply-chain-planning, microservices/production-planning, microservices/warehouse with module tag `SCM/APO` and keeps data ownership inside the destination services.

### §D-4 ABAP-equivalent extensibility
oyatie does not create a proprietary ABAP-equivalent scripting language. Extensibility uses plugin-app-store for distribution, developer-sdk for typed APIs, workflow-studio for visual authoring, workflow-engine for deterministic execution, Cedar fragments for authorization, and ontology actions for object mutations.
Tenant extensions are versioned, audited, constrained by cell tier, and deployable through marketplace DealSets when sold or licensed to other tenants.

### §D-5 Ontology mapping
Every SAP business-object type maps to an oyatie ontology object type. Tenants may subclass object types without breaking the canonical import/export contract. Subclassing never removes required audit, retention, or policy fields.
- Ontology object type `BusinessPartner` maps from SAP and competitor imports with tenant subclassing, source-system provenance, and audit-chain sealing.

### §D-6 Migration path from SAP
Migration starts with connect adapter registration, source-system inventory, SAP table/export classification, data-class mapping, tenant authority confirmation, and read-only dry run. Bulk import produces per-table migration plans with row counts, rejected rows, transform digests, and rollback envelopes.
- SAP table family BKPF/BSEG: connect extracts, validates checksums, maps to ontology object types, stages tenant-scoped rows, runs referential-integrity checks, emits dry-run evidence, then imports by reversible batch.
- SAP table family KNA1: connect extracts, validates checksums, maps to ontology object types, stages tenant-scoped rows, runs referential-integrity checks, emits dry-run evidence, then imports by reversible batch.

### §D-7 ERP-grade data integrity
ERP-grade data integrity requires multi-tenant transaction isolation, per-document audit-chain, idempotent command handling, explicit eventual-consistency tradeoffs, and signed migration evidence. Local invariants use service-owned transactions. Cross-service invariants use workflow-engine sagas with compensating actions and audit-chain evidence.
Hard financial postings, inventory movements, treasury commitments, and compliance holds cannot be hidden behind best-effort events. They require idempotency keys, lock scopes, reversal events, and explicit stale-read indicators.

## §E Implementation Footprint

### §E.PP microservices/production-planning/ buildout sequence
1. Anchor scaffold: manifest, PRD, architecture, and compliance docs.
2. Contract slice: OpenAPI 3.2.0 command contract, AsyncAPI 3.1.0 event contract, proto3 service contract.
3. Domain slice: bounded context kernel and ontology object mappings.
4. Policy slice: Cedar fragments, data-class matrix, and compliance-pack activation.
5. Workflow slice: approval, exception, reversal, and migration workflows.
6. Integration slice: connect adapters and marketplace/DealSet hooks.
7. Observability slice: metrics, traces, logs, audit events, dashboards, SLOs.
8. Full PR-143 suite: runbooks, threat model, DPIA, scorecards, IaC, catalog, capability records, and implementation plans.

4. Policy slice: Cedar fragments, data-class matrix, and compliance-pack activation.
5. Workflow slice: approval, exception, reversal, and migration workflows.
6. Integration slice: connect adapters and marketplace/DealSet hooks.
7. Observability slice: metrics, traces, logs, audit events, dashboards, SLOs.
8. Full PR-143 suite: runbooks, threat model, DPIA, scorecards, IaC, catalog, capability records, and implementation plans.

## §F Migration
Tenants migrating from SAP, Oracle, Workday, NetSuite, Sage Intacct, Infor, or IFS receive a source-system inventory, object mapping, dry-run import, reconciliation report, parallel run, cutover window, and rollback envelope. Identity continuity is preserved: user principals, supplier/customer identities, company codes, cost centers, and audit references map into tenant-scoped ontology objects rather than being recreated as unrelated accounts.
SAP migration uses table-family extraction and business-object reconciliation. Oracle Fusion migration uses REST and BI Publisher exports. Workday migration uses report-as-a-service and payroll/finance connectors. NetSuite migration uses SuiteTalk and CSV exports. Sage, Infor, and IFS use connector-specific export adapters.

## §H Change Log and Naming Justifications
- 2026-05-20: Proposed ERP parity doctrine and authorized nine anchor scaffolds.
- `erp-coverage`: chosen for the product-level PRD because it describes a coverage doctrine, not an ERP product bundle.
- `production-planning`, `quality-management`, `plant-maintenance`, `warehouse`, `real-estate`, `crm`, `treasury`, `supply-chain-planning`, and `global-trade`: chosen because each maps to a distinct ERP concern with independent SLO, deployment, policy, and scaling boundaries.
- `erp.*` action namespace is rejected. Actions remain service-owned, for example `treasury.cash_position.calculate` or `warehouse.pick_wave.release`.
- `EVT-ERP-PARITY-MAP-CHANGED` is reserved for updates to the doctrine itself; module services emit their own domain events.
- §H.1 ERP parity invariant for FI: destination specs/microservices/accounting.json + microservices/payments + microservices/finops-portal + microservices/treasury must preserve tenant scope, Cedar authorization, ontology mapping, workflow evidence, audit-chain sealing, migration provenance, and no-grouping ownership before claiming SAP-parity coverage.
- §H.2 ERP parity invariant for CO: destination microservices/finops-portal + microservices/ontology + microservices/workflow-engine + microservices/supply-chain-planning must preserve tenant scope, Cedar authorization, ontology mapping, workflow evidence, audit-chain sealing, migration provenance, and no-grouping ownership before claiming SAP-parity coverage.
